\end{verbatim}}
\subsection*{Arguments.SolaFide.Dispute — who prevails over sola fide}
The results in \texttt{Results.lean} ask what each package entails. This module asks what happens when the positions meet: the three Reformed strands, each argued alone; Trent; the apocalyptic reading of Martyn and Campbell; Sanders and Dunn on covenantal nomism, and the critics who answer them; and Jervell on the yoke of Acts 15. Who defeats whom is not stipulated — each defeat, and each absence of one, is a theorem about the positions' premises (see \texttt{Testimony.Logic.Dispute}).

\subsubsection*{The ratings decide nothing here}
Every party's weakest link is \texttt{disputed}: each Reformed strand's inference to faith alone is the one Trent's canon 9 denies, and each holds the Reformed distinction between justification and sanctification, which Trent anathematises; Trent rests on its own definition of justification, which Westminster denies; the apocalyptic reading denies an atom (and a denial ranks at the bottom); and so on. So no rating blocks any attack, and the outcome is fixed entirely by who contradicts whom. That is unlike the dispute over Isaiah 7:14, where one inference rated \texttt{plausible} carried the verdict.

\subsubsection*{Who defeats whom}
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Trent and each Reformed strand defeat each other.} Trent denies "not by
\end{itemize}
  works"; each strand concludes it. Trent also undermines each strand: its   definition of justification — the renewal of the inward man — contradicts the   distinction between justification and sanctification that each strand holds.   That is where the objection comes from: with the distinction in place of the   definition, Trent's case no longer denies "not by works"   (\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}objection\_\allowbreak{}rests\_\allowbreak{}on\_\allowbreak{}its\_\allowbreak{}definition}).

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{The apocalyptic reading and Paul defeat each other}: it denies the objective
\end{itemize}
  genitive, and Paul concludes faith alone. \textbf{Luke and Acts defeat it}, and it   defeats neither — it contradicts nothing they rest on.

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{The apocalyptic reading defeats Trent, and Trent does not defeat it.} They
\end{itemize}
  agree that justification is not by faith alone; but the apocalyptic reading   holds "not by works" — God's deliverance is conditioned on nothing a person   does — and nothing Trent holds contradicts its grounds.

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Sanders defeats Paul}, by denying the ἔργα νόμου premise; Paul and the
\end{itemize}
  critics each defeat Sanders back.

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Jervell and Acts defeat each other}, over the yoke.
\end{itemize}
\subsubsection*{What follows}
\textbf{Nothing prevails outright} (\texttt{nothing\_\allowbreak{}prevails\_\allowbreak{}over\_\allowbreak{}sola\_\allowbreak{}fide}): every party is defeated by someone, and the grounded extension is empty.

\textbf{But sola fide from Luke 7:50 is accepted on every resolution} (\texttt{dominical\_\allowbreak{}case\_\allowbreak{}skeptically\_\allowbreak{}accepted}). Trent cannot be defended — the apocalyptic reading defeats it, and whatever answers the apocalyptic reading also defeats Trent (\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}indefensible}). Nor can the apocalyptic reading — the dominical case defeats it, and only Trent could answer the dominical case (\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}indefensible}). So every maximal defensible position holds the dominical case.

\subsubsection*{What the verdict rests on}
Three things, each stated as a result or a limitation rather than left implicit.

\textbf{The two rivals answering each other.} Remove the apocalyptic reading and Trent defends itself against the three strands; sola fide is then accepted on some resolutions and not others (\texttt{sola\_\allowbreak{}fide\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}forced\_\allowbreak{}without\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}reading}). The verdict is not that sola fide answers Trent. It is that Trent cannot answer a rival that agrees with it about faith and disagrees with it about works.

\textbf{An absence.} The dominical case is attacked by no one but Trent, because no source cited here argues that σῴζω at Luke 7:50 means healing. That absence is now also its rating: the premise is \texttt{well\allowbreak{}Supported}, not \texttt{disputed}, because a search for a scholar arguing the healing sense \emph{at 7:50} found none, and at 7:50 there is no illness — the saying follows "your sins are forgiven". It is not \texttt{consensus}, because the same formula means "made you well" at Luke 8:48, 17:19 and 18:42. A cited argument for the healing reading would attack the dominical case, lower the rating, and could change the verdict.

\textbf{An objection answered in advance.} "Her sins are forgiven, for she loved much" (7:47) is the text a rival would use to make love, not faith, the ground of her forgiveness. The dominical case does not leave that open: it carries \texttt{luke7\_\allowbreak{}47\allowbreak{}Love\allowbreak{}Is\allowbreak{}Evidence} — her love is the evidence of forgiveness, not its ground, as 7:47b and the parable of 7:41–43 read it — as a premise of its own. It is Padilla's reading — she "loves much because she has been forgiven much" — and Melanchthon's in the \emph{Apology of the Augsburg Confession}: "Christ interprets Himself when He adds: Thy faith hath saved thee", so the woman did not merit forgiveness "by that work of love". It is rated \texttt{plausible}, because Trent counts beginning to love God among the dispositions to justification (Session VI, ch. 6); but no party here argues that reading of 7:47.

\textbf{The parties chosen.} A dispute is over the arguments put into it. Hays's subjective genitive is represented through the apocalyptic reading, which holds it; a Hays party of its own, the \emph{Joint Declaration}, and a party for the Finnish reading would each be new arguments to weigh.

\paragraph*{The positions}
\leavevmode\par
Each party is an argument in its own right: premises that can hold together, a conclusion they deliver, and an inference someone has rated. The Reformed case enters as three parties, one per strand, because a dispute weighs arguments whole: one package holding all three strands would be defeated by a contradiction of any one of them, and the redundancy the results prove would count for nothing.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{reformed\allowbreak{}Ontology}}\par\nobreak\noindent
What the Reformed hold about what justification \emph{is}, where Trent is heard: justification and sanctification are distinct, and sanctification follows. It carries no strand's conclusion — the strands reach sola fide without it — but it is the premise Trent's definition contradicts, so it belongs to each Reformed party, and Trent's attack on each lands on it as well as on "not by works". Not added to \texttt{reformed}, because the other packages built from it, the Finnish reading among them, would then hold it on their authors' behalf.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{32} \\
\end{align*}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{pauline\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Sola fide from Paul (Galatians 2:16)\par\nobreak\noindent
The Pauline strand, argued alone: sola fide from Galatians 2:16.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{8} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{6} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{7} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & P_{4} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{17} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{9} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{20} \\
  \text{(12)} \quad & P_{21} \\
  \text{(13)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(14)} \quad & P_{32} \\
  \text{(15)} \quad & (P_{2} \land P_{3} \land P_{8} \land P_{7} \land P_{6}) \rightarrow P_{24} \\
  \text{(16)} \quad & (P_{20} \land P_{21}) \rightarrow P_{22} \\
  \text{(17)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5}) \rightarrow P_{25} \\
  \text{(18)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5} \land P_{22} \land P_{23}) \rightarrow P_{26} \\
  \text{(19)} \quad & (P_{24} \land P_{1}) \rightarrow P_{27} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{25} \land P_{26} \land P_{27}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{dominical\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Sola fide from Jesus' words (Luke 7:50)\par\nobreak\noindent
The dominical strand, argued alone: sola fide from Luke 7:50.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{18} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{19} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{4} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & P_{17} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{9} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{20} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{21} \\
  \text{(12)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(13)} \quad & P_{32} \\
  \text{(14)} \quad & (P_{17} \land P_{18} \land P_{19}) \rightarrow P_{24} \\
  \text{(15)} \quad & (P_{20} \land P_{21}) \rightarrow P_{22} \\
  \text{(16)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5}) \rightarrow P_{25} \\
  \text{(17)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5} \land P_{22} \land P_{23}) \rightarrow P_{26} \\
  \text{(18)} \quad & (P_{24} \land P_{1}) \rightarrow P_{27} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{25} \land P_{26} \land P_{27}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apostolic\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Sola fide from Peter (Acts 15:9–11)\par\nobreak\noindent
The apostolic strand, argued alone: sola fide from Peter at Jerusalem.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{10} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{4} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & P_{17} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & P_{9} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{20} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{21} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(12)} \quad & P_{32} \\
  \text{(13)} \quad & (P_{9} \land P_{10}) \rightarrow P_{24} \\
  \text{(14)} \quad & (P_{20} \land P_{21}) \rightarrow P_{22} \\
  \text{(15)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5}) \rightarrow P_{25} \\
  \text{(16)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5} \land P_{22} \land P_{23}) \rightarrow P_{26} \\
  \text{(17)} \quad & (P_{24} \land P_{1}) \rightarrow P_{27} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{25} \land P_{26} \land P_{27}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{tridentine\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Trent, against 'not by works'\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent, as the argument it makes: justification is the renewal of the inward man, that renewal grows through good works, so works done in grace merit an increase of justification, and salvation is not apart from works.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{31} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & P_{33} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & (P_{31} \land P_{33}) \rightarrow P_{30} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{31} \rightarrow \lnot P_{28} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{31} \rightarrow \lnot P_{32} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{30} \rightarrow \lnot P_{26} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{26}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Apocalyptic reading, against faith as the condition\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic reading, as the argument it makes: faith is not the condition of justification. It keeps grace and "not by works", by its own step.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & \lnot P_{7} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{16} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{8} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & (\lnot P_{7} \land P_{16}) \rightarrow \lnot P_{24} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{16} \rightarrow P_{25} \land P_{26} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & (P_{24} \land P_{1}) \rightarrow P_{27} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{24}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{sanders\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Covenantal nomism (Sanders, Dunn)\par\nobreak\noindent
Sanders and Dunn: covenantal nomism, so ἔργα νόμου are the boundary markers.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{29} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{29} \rightarrow \lnot P_{6} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{6}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{critics\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Variegated nomism (Gathercole, Carson et al.)\par\nobreak\noindent
Gathercole and \emph{Variegated Nomism}: not covenantal nomism, so ἔργα νόμου are works in general.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & \lnot P_{29} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{8} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & (\lnot P_{29} \land P_{8}) \rightarrow P_{6} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{6}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{jervell\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Law-observant Luke (Jervell)\par\nobreak\noindent
Jervell: Luke is law-observant, so the yoke is not the law as a condition of salvation.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{11} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{11} \rightarrow \lnot P_{10} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{10}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\paragraph*{Each position holds together and delivers its conclusion}
\leavevmode\par

The dominical case has a model.

The apostolic case delivers sola fide.

-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound

The apostolic case has a model.

-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{tridentine\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}establishes}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's case delivers its conclusion: salvation is not apart from works.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem tridentineCase_establishes : Establishes tridentineCase
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{tridentine\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}is\_\allowbreak{}satisfiable}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's case has a model.

-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{Where Trent's objection comes from}
\leavevmode\par
Trent's objection to "not by works" is not a separate thesis about works. It follows from what Trent says justification \emph{is}: the renewal of the inward man, not remission of sins only (ch. 7). Grant that, grant that the renewal grows as the justified do good works (ch. 10), and good works increase justification (canon 24), which denies "not by works" (ch. 16). The Reformed grant the growth — Westminster XIII.1 calls it sanctification — and deny the definition.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{tridentine\allowbreak{}Case\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}The\allowbreak{}Reformed\allowbreak{}Distinction}} --- Trent, on the Reformed distinction\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's case with its definition of justification exchanged for the Reformed distinction: everything else Trent holds, including that the renewal grows through good works, and justification and sanctification distinct.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{33} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & (P_{31} \land P_{33}) \rightarrow P_{30} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & P_{31} \rightarrow \lnot P_{28} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{31} \rightarrow \lnot P_{32} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{30} \rightarrow \lnot P_{26} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{32} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{26}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{reformed\allowbreak{}Distinction\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's world, with the Reformed distinction in place of its definition: the renewal grows, but it is sanctification, so it increases nothing called justification, and salvation is not by works.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def reformedDistinctionReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.justificationIncludesSanctification => False
    | Claim.worksMeritIncreaseOfJustification => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}objection\_\allowbreak{}rests\_\allowbreak{}on\_\allowbreak{}its\_\allowbreak{}definition}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent's objection rests on its definition of justification.} Keep all of Trent's case except its definition, grant the Reformed distinction instead, and "not apart from works" no longer follows — though both sides still hold that the renewal of the justified grows through good works. So the dispute over works is, at bottom, a dispute over what justification \emph{is}: whether the renewal that grows is justification or the sanctification that follows it.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_objection_rests_on_its_definition : ¬Establishes
    tridentineCaseOnTheReformedDistinction
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}establishes}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic case delivers its conclusion.

{\small\begin{verbatim}

-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}is\_\allowbreak{}satisfiable}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic case has a model.

{\small\begin{verbatim}

\end{verbatim}}

\paragraph*{Strength: every position's weakest link is \texttt{disputed}}
\leavevmode\par
Every party rests on something cited \texttt{disputed}, or denies an atom, which ranks at the bottom; so every party's strength is \texttt{0}, and no rating blocks any attack. In this dispute the ratings decide nothing, and the verdict is fixed by who contradicts whom.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{pauline\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The Pauline case rests on its lexical premises, cited \texttt{disputed}, as well as on the Reformed distinction.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem paulineCase_strength : paulineCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{dominical\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The dominical case rests on the Reformed distinction, cited \texttt{disputed}, and on an inference Trent's canon 9 denies. Its own two premises rank higher: σῴζω \texttt{well\allowbreak{}Supported}, and the reading of 7:47 \texttt{plausible}.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem dominicalCase_strength : dominicalCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apostolic\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apostolic case rests on the yoke premise, cited \texttt{disputed}.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apostolicCase_strength : apostolicCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{tridentine\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent rests on its definition of justification, cited \texttt{disputed}.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem tridentineCase_strength : tridentineCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic case denies the objective genitive.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalypticCase_strength : apocalypticCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{sanders\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Sanders' case rests on covenantal nomism, cited \texttt{disputed}.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem sandersCase_strength : sandersCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{critics\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The critics' case denies covenantal nomism.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem criticsCase_strength : criticsCase.strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{jervell\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Jervell's step is contested, by Bruce.

{\small\begin{verbatim}

-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{The defeats}
\leavevmode\par
Each is decided from the two packages' premises by \texttt{Horn.defeats?} and checked by the kernel: the attack, and the comparison of strengths.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}pauline}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent defeats the Pauline case.} It entails the denial of "not by works", so it rebuts sola fide.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_defeats_pauline : Defeats tridentineCase paulineCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}dominical}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent defeats the dominical case}, on the same rebuttal.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_defeats_dominical : Defeats tridentineCase dominicalCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}apostolic}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent defeats the apostolic case}, on the same rebuttal.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_defeats_apostolic : Defeats tridentineCase apostolicCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{pauline\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}trent}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The Pauline case defeats Trent back.} It entails "not by works", which Trent denies.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem pauline_defeats_trent : Defeats paulineCase tridentineCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{dominical\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}trent}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The dominical case defeats Trent back.}

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem dominical_defeats_trent : Defeats dominicalCase tridentineCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apostolic\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}trent}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The apostolic case defeats Trent back.}

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apostolic_defeats_trent : Defeats apostolicCase tridentineCase

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}defeats\_\allowbreak{}trent}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The apocalyptic reading defeats Trent.} It holds "not by works" by its own step — God's deliverance is conditioned on nothing a person does — and Trent denies it.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_defeats_trent : Defeats apocalypticCase tridentineCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}

theorem apostolic_defeats_jervell : Defeats apostolicCase jervellCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{What does not defeat}
\leavevmode\par
Most pairs of parties are compatible: some world holds both, and seven such worlds are named below (\texttt{Dispute.Stand\allowbreak{}Together}) — the readings on which whole groups of positions can be held at once. Three pairs conflict in one direction only. All of them, like every cell of the table, are decided by \texttt{Horn.defeats?} from the premises.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{critics\allowbreak{}Jervell\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The critics' world with Jervell's: covenantal nomism fails, and the yoke is Israel's law for gentiles. The Pauline and dominical cases hold in it.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def criticsJervellReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.secondTempleCovenantalNomism => False
    | Claim.acts15YokeIsLawAsCondition => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\allowbreak{}Sanders\allowbreak{}Jervell\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's world with Sanders' and Jervell's.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def trentSandersJervellReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.salvationNotByWorks => False
    | Claim.justificationIsForensicOnly => False
    | Claim.worksOfLawMeansWorksGenerally => False
    | Claim.acts15YokeIsLawAsCondition => False
    | Claim.justificationDistinctFromSanctification => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\allowbreak{}Critics\allowbreak{}Jervell\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's world with the critics' and Jervell's.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def trentCriticsJervellReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.salvationNotByWorks => False
    | Claim.justificationIsForensicOnly => False
    | Claim.secondTempleCovenantalNomism => False
    | Claim.acts15YokeIsLawAsCondition => False
    | Claim.justificationDistinctFromSanctification => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Sanders\allowbreak{}Jervell\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic world with Sanders' and Jervell's.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def apocalypticSandersJervellReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.pistisChristouObjective => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | Claim.worksOfLawMeansWorksGenerally => False
    | Claim.acts15YokeIsLawAsCondition => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Critics\allowbreak{}Jervell\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic world with the critics' and Jervell's.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def apocalypticCriticsJervellReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.pistisChristouObjective => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | Claim.secondTempleCovenantalNomism => False
    | Claim.acts15YokeIsLawAsCondition => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}does\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}defeat\_\allowbreak{}dominical}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The apocalyptic reading does not defeat the dominical case.} It contradicts nothing the dominical case rests on — not Luke 7:50, not σῴζω, not the reading of 7:47 — and it does not deny its conclusion: it grants grace and "not by works", and it can grant that salvation is received through faith while denying that faith is its condition. Why, premise by premise: \texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}sola\_\allowbreak{}fide\_\allowbreak{}as\_\allowbreak{}stated} and \texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}dominical\_\allowbreak{}step\_\allowbreak{}by\_\allowbreak{}healing}.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_does_not_defeat_dominical : ¬Defeats apocalypticCase
    dominicalCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}does\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}defeat\_\allowbreak{}apostolic}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Nor the apostolic case}, for the same reasons (\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apostolic\_\allowbreak{}step\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}jervell}).

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_does_not_defeat_apostolic : ¬Defeats apocalypticCase
    apostolicCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}does\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}defeat\_\allowbreak{}apocalyptic}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent does not defeat the apocalyptic reading.} They agree on its conclusion — justification is not by faith alone — and Trent contradicts none of its premises: nothing Trent holds settles the genitive, or what δικαιοσύνη θεοῦ names (\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}subjective\_\allowbreak{}genitive}, \texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}conclusion}). So the apocalyptic reading's "not by works" stands against Trent unanswered.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_does_not_defeat_apocalyptic : ¬Defeats tridentineCase
    apocalypticCase
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{Why these are not counters}
\leavevmode\par
The three absences above are computed, and a computation names no reason. The readings below do: each is a way of holding one position that leaves one premise or conclusion of another standing, and each result checks that the reading holds everything the first position holds as well.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}With\allowbreak{}Faith\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic world in which salvation is still received through faith — faith is not the condition, but it is the means. Grace and "not by works" hold.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def apocalypticWithFaithReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.pistisChristouObjective => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}sola\_\allowbreak{}fide\_\allowbreak{}as\_\allowbreak{}stated}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The apocalyptic reading grants what the Reformed strands conclude.} Grace, "not by works", and salvation received through faith all hold in its world; what it denies is that faith is the condition. So it rebuts neither Luke's case nor Acts'.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_grants_sola_fide_as_stated : Grants apocalypticCase
    dominicalCase.conclusion
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Healing\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic world, with Jesus' σέσωκέν σε read as healing.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def apocalypticHealingReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.pistisChristouObjective => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | Claim.sozoIsSoteriological => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}dominical\_\allowbreak{}step\_\allowbreak{}by\_\allowbreak{}healing}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The apocalyptic reader can grant the dominical step}, by reading σέσωκέν σε at Luke 7:50 as healing rather than salvation: the step then has a ground that fails, and holds without delivering faith alone. So the apocalyptic reading does not undermine the dominical case on its step — it answers Luke only by disputing the lexical premise, which is where the dominical case is open.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_grants_the_dominical_step_by_healing : Grants
    apocalypticCase dominicalToFaithAlone
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\allowbreak{}Jervell\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic world, with the yoke read as Jervell reads it.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def apocalypticJervellReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.pistisChristouObjective => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | Claim.acts15YokeIsLawAsCondition => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apostolic\_\allowbreak{}step\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}jervell}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The apocalyptic reader can grant the apostolic step}, by reading the yoke of Acts 15:10 as Jervell does — Israel's law for gentiles, not the law as a condition of salvation.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_grants_the_apostolic_step_with_jervell : Grants
    apocalypticCase apostolicToFaithAlone
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\allowbreak{}Without\allowbreak{}Deliverance\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's world, with the objective genitive and the apocalyptic reading of δικαιοσύνη θεοῦ both denied.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def trentWithoutDeliveranceReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.salvationNotByWorks => False
    | Claim.justificationIsForensicOnly => False
    | Claim.pistisChristouObjective => False
    | Claim.righteousnessOfGodIsDeliverance => False
    | Claim.justificationDistinctFromSanctification => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}subjective\_\allowbreak{}genitive}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent can grant the subjective genitive.} Nothing Trent holds settles how πίστις Χριστοῦ is read, so the apocalyptic reading's denial of the objective genitive stands against Trent unanswered.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_grants_the_subjective_genitive : Grants tridentineCase (notP
    Claim.pistisChristouObjective)
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\allowbreak{}Without\allowbreak{}Faith\allowbreak{}Alone\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent's world, in which justification is not by faith alone — the apocalyptic reading's conclusion, which Trent shares.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def trentWithoutFaithAloneReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.salvationNotByWorks => False
    | Claim.justificationIsForensicOnly => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | Claim.justificationDistinctFromSanctification => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}grants\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}conclusion}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent grants the apocalyptic reading's conclusion}: justification is not by faith alone. They agree on it for different reasons, so Trent does not rebut the apocalyptic reading.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_grants_the_apocalyptic_conclusion : Grants tridentineCase
    apocalypticCase.conclusion
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{The dispute}
\leavevmode\par
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{party\allowbreak{}Node}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The package each party argues from.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def partyNode : Party → ArgumentPackage Claim :=
  fun x =>
    match x with
    | Party.pauline => paulineCase
    | Party.dominical => dominicalCase
    | Party.apostolic => apostolicCase
    | Party.trent => tridentineCase
    | Party.apocalyptic => apocalypticCase
    | Party.sanders => sandersCase
    | Party.critics => criticsCase
    | Party.jervell => jervellCase
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{sola\allowbreak{}Fide\allowbreak{}Dispute}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The dispute over sola fide: every party's premises have a model, every party establishes its conclusion, and every party's inferences are rated.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def solaFideDispute : Dispute Claim Party :=
  { node := partyNode,
    consistent := solaFideDispute._proof_1,
    sound := solaFideDispute._proof_2,
    rated := solaFideDispute._proof_3 }
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{reformed\_\allowbreak{}strands\_\allowbreak{}stand\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}critics}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The Reformed strands and the critics stand together, where covenantal nomism fails and everything else holds.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem reformed_strands_stand_with_the_critics :
    solaFideDispute.StandTogether [Party.pauline, Party.dominical,
    Party.apostolic, Party.critics]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{paul\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}luke\_\allowbreak{}stand\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}jervell}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Paul and Luke stand with the critics and with Jervell.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem paul_and_luke_stand_with_jervell : solaFideDispute.StandTogether
    [Party.pauline, Party.dominical, Party.jervell, Party.critics]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luke\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}acts\_\allowbreak{}stand\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}sanders}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Luke and Acts stand with Sanders: neither rests on ἔργα νόμου.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem luke_and_acts_stand_with_sanders : solaFideDispute.StandTogether
    [Party.dominical, Party.apostolic, Party.sanders]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}stands\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}sanders\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}jervell}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent stands with Sanders and Jervell.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_stands_with_sanders_and_jervell : solaFideDispute.StandTogether
    [Party.trent, Party.sanders, Party.jervell]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}stands\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}critics\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}jervell}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Trent stands with the critics and Jervell.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_stands_with_the_critics_and_jervell :
    solaFideDispute.StandTogether [Party.trent, Party.critics, Party.jervell]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}stands\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}sanders\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}jervell}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic reading stands with Sanders and Jervell.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_stands_with_sanders_and_jervell :
    solaFideDispute.StandTogether [Party.apocalyptic, Party.sanders,
    Party.jervell]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}stands\_\allowbreak{}with\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}critics\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}jervell}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The apocalyptic reading stands with the critics and Jervell.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_stands_with_the_critics_and_jervell :
    solaFideDispute.StandTogether [Party.apocalyptic, Party.critics,
    Party.jervell]
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{party\allowbreak{}Defeats}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The defeats of the dispute, as a table.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def partyDefeats : Party → Party → Prop
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{inst\allowbreak{}Decidable\allowbreak{}Rel\allowbreak{}Party\allowbreak{}Party\allowbreak{}Defeats}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The table is finite, so membership in it is decidable.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def instDecidableRelPartyPartyDefeats : DecidableRel partyDefeats
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{party\allowbreak{}Node\_\allowbreak{}strength}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Every party's weakest link ranks at the bottom.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem partyNode_strength : ∀ (i : Party), (partyNode i).strength = 0
-- axioms: propext
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{sola\allowbreak{}Fide\allowbreak{}Dispute\_\allowbreak{}defeats}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Who defeats whom}, all sixty-four pairs. Trent and each Reformed strand defeat each other. The apocalyptic reading and Paul defeat each other; Luke and Acts defeat it, and it defeats neither; it defeats Trent, and Trent does not defeat it. Sanders defeats Paul and the critics, and both defeat him back. Jervell and Acts defeat each other. Nothing else.

Every cell is computed from the parties' premises by \texttt{Horn.defeats?} and checked by the kernel — the defeats, and the absences of defeat, alike. The table above is what the computation is checked against; none of it is assumed.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem solaFideDispute_defeats : ∀ (i j : Party), solaFideDispute.defeats i j
    ↔ partyDefeats i j
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{sola\allowbreak{}Fide\allowbreak{}Finite}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The dispute in the form the verdict solver computes with: every party, and the table.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def solaFideFinite : Solver.Finite solaFideDispute.defeats :=
  {
    parties :=
      [Party.pauline, Party.dominical,
        Party.apostolic, Party.trent,
        Party.apocalyptic, Party.sanders,
        Party.critics, Party.jervell],
    complete := solaFideFinite._proof_1,
    defeats := fun i j => decide (partyDefeats i j),
    spec := solaFideFinite._proof_2 }
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{What the dispute decides}
\leavevmode\par
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{nothing\_\allowbreak{}prevails\_\allowbreak{}over\_\allowbreak{}sola\_\allowbreak{}fide}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Nothing prevails outright.} Every party is defeated by some other: each Reformed strand by Trent, Trent by each strand and by the apocalyptic reading, the apocalyptic reading by Paul, Sanders by the critics, the critics by Sanders, Jervell by Acts. The grounded extension — what the dispute forces, before any choice between rivals — is empty.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem nothing_prevails_over_sola_fide : Framework.grounded
    solaFideDispute.defeats = ∅
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{trent\_\allowbreak{}indefensible}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Trent cannot be defended.} The apocalyptic reading defeats it — it holds "not by works" on grounds Trent does not contradict — and the only parties that defeat the apocalyptic reading are the Reformed strands, each of which also defeats Trent. No position can hold Trent and answer the apocalyptic reading at once.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem trent_indefensible : ∀ (S : Set Party), Framework.Admissible
    solaFideDispute.defeats S → Party.trent ∉ S
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}indefensible}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Nor can the apocalyptic reading.} The dominical case defeats it, and the only party that defeats the dominical case is Trent — which the apocalyptic reading itself defeats.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem apocalyptic_indefensible : ∀ (S : Set Party), Framework.Admissible
    solaFideDispute.defeats S → Party.apocalyptic ∉ S
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{dominical\_\allowbreak{}case\_\allowbreak{}skeptically\_\allowbreak{}accepted}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Sola fide from Luke 7:50 is accepted on every resolution.} Its only defeater is Trent, which no admissible position can hold; it defeats back the only other party it conflicts with, the apocalyptic reading, which no admissible position can hold either. So every preferred extension — every maximal defensible position — contains it.

This is the dispute's verdict, and it is narrower than it sounds. It is not reached by the grounded semantics, which forces nothing (\texttt{nothing\_\allowbreak{}prevails\_\allowbreak{}over\_\allowbreak{}sola\_\allowbreak{}fide}). It rests on the two rivals to sola fide defeating each other: remove the apocalyptic reading and Trent can be defended again (\texttt{sola\_\allowbreak{}fide\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}forced\_\allowbreak{}without\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}reading}). And the dominical case is attacked by no one but Trent because no source cited here argues that σῴζω at Luke 7:50 means healing, and none argues that the woman's love at 7:47 was the ground of her forgiveness.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem dominical_case_skeptically_accepted : Framework.SkepticallyAccepted
    solaFideDispute.defeats Party.dominical
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{without\allowbreak{}Apocalyptic}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The dispute without the apocalyptic reading.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def withoutApocalyptic : Dispute Claim { i // i ≠ Party.apocalyptic } :=
  solaFideDispute.restrict fun x =>
    x ≠ Party.apocalyptic
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{sola\_\allowbreak{}fide\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}forced\_\allowbreak{}without\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}apocalyptic\_\allowbreak{}reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Without the apocalyptic reading, sola fide is no longer forced.} Trent, heard against the three Reformed strands alone, defends itself: it defeats each of them back. So some maximal defensible position holds Trent, and that position cannot hold the dominical case, which Trent defeats.

The verdict of \texttt{dominical\_\allowbreak{}case\_\allowbreak{}skeptically\_\allowbreak{}accepted} therefore rests on one rival to sola fide answering the other: on the apocalyptic reading holding "not by works" where Trent denies it.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem sola_fide_not_forced_without_the_apocalyptic_reading :
    ¬Framework.SkepticallyAccepted withoutApocalyptic.defeats
    ⟨Party.dominical, ⋯⟩
-- axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\subsection*{Arguments.SolaFide.Johannine — "this is the work of God, that you believe"}
A fourth strand for sola fide, from John's Gospel, encoded on its own. Asked what they must do "to be doing the works of God", the crowd is told: "This is the work of God, that you believe in him whom he has sent" (John 6:28–29). The question of works is put to Jesus in terms, and he answers with believing. The Gospel says the same throughout: eternal life, and passing out of judgement, through believing in the Son (3:16–18, 3:36, 5:24), and it states its own purpose in those terms (20:31).

\subsubsection*{The hinge: what believing is}
Both sides grant the verse. What divides them is what its believing is.

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Calvin} reads it as trust. Christ calls faith a work only improperly:
\end{itemize}
  faith "brings nothing to God, but, on the contrary, places man before God as   empty and poor, that he may be filled with Christ", and "bestows on man no   other righteousness than that which he receives from Christ". It is "a   passive work, to which no reward can be paid".

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Aquinas} reads it as faith formed by charity. Paul distinguishes faith
\end{itemize}
  "only from external works"; to believe \emph{in} him (\emph{in illum}), as one's end,   "is proper to faith living through the love of charity", and faith so living   "is the principle of all our good works" (\emph{Super Ioannem} 6, lect. 3, n. 901).   That is Trent's account of justifying faith (Session VI, ch. 7): faith   without hope and charity "neither unites man perfectly with Christ, nor makes   him a living member of His body".

So the strand has the same shape as the others: shared texts, and a reading on which everything turns. Grant Aquinas his reading and the texts no longer deliver faith alone (\texttt{johannine\_\allowbreak{}strand\_\allowbreak{}rests\_\allowbreak{}on\_\allowbreak{}believing\_\allowbreak{}as\_\allowbreak{}trust}).

\subsubsection*{John has no word for justification}
δικαιόω does not occur in John. The strand's step therefore runs from eternal life through believing to justification by faith alone, and that is an inference, not a reading of a word. It is cited to Calvin, whose comment on 6:29 speaks of the righteousness faith receives from Christ, and rated \texttt{plausible}: no cited source grants the strand's grounds and denies that step, but none argues the bridge from John's vocabulary to Paul's at length either.

\subsubsection*{Luther's answer, from Galatians 3}
Aquinas's reading is not left standing unanswered. Luther meets it head-on in his lectures on Galatians. At 3:11: "The scholastics misconstrue this passage by saying: 'The just shall live by faith, if it is a working faith, or a faith formed and performed by charitable works.' … To speak of formed or unformed faith, a sort of double faith, is contrary to the Scriptures." At 3:12: "Does not the Law command charity? … If the law requires charity, charity is part of the Law and not of faith." So a faith that justifies because charity forms it justifies by the law after all — and "the law is not of faith" (Galatians 3:12).

The argument is encoded as a line of its own (\texttt{luther\allowbreak{}Line}). Its grounds are both things Aquinas grants: the text of Galatians 3:11–12, and that love of God and neighbour is what the law commands (Deuteronomy 6:5). Its step is Luther's: from those, the believing that justifies is not faith formed by charity. Granted the step, Aquinas's reading of John 6:29 falls (\texttt{luther\_\allowbreak{}answers\_\allowbreak{}aquinas\_\allowbreak{}from\_\allowbreak{}galatians}). But the step is the whole of the answer: the texts alone do not exclude his reading (\texttt{luther\_\allowbreak{}answer\_\allowbreak{}rests\_\allowbreak{}on\_\allowbreak{}his\_\allowbreak{}step}), and the step is rated \texttt{disputed}, because Aquinas grants both of its grounds and denies its conclusion. Nor does removing Aquinas's reading establish Calvin's: that the believing of 6:29 is not formed by charity is weaker than that it is trust.

Calvin makes the neighbouring argument in the \emph{Institutes} (III.xi.19), which the library already cites for \texttt{works\allowbreak{}Of\allowbreak{}Law\allowbreak{}Means\allowbreak{}Works\allowbreak{}Generally}: against those who "pretend that the works excluded are ceremonial, not moral works", he cites Galatians 3:10 and 3:12 — the curse on everyone who does not continue in \emph{all} that the law commands — and concludes that "the whole Law is spoken of when the power of justifying is denied to it". Aquinas's distinction is between external and inward works rather than ceremonial and moral ones, so it is Luther's argument on 3:11–12, not Calvin's, that meets him directly.

The Catholic reply rests on Galatians 5:6, "faith working through love", which Trent cites (Session VI, ch. 7). How Luther and Calvin read that verse is not yet encoded.

\subsubsection*{What is not yet done}
The strand is encoded alone, as a package of its own. It is \textbf{not} yet part of \texttt{reformed} or of the sola fide dispute, and that is deliberate. Adding a fourth strand to \texttt{reformed} changes the joint results — no premise removed from the other three strands defeats sola fide while John's strand stands — and a Johannine party in \texttt{Dispute.lean} changes the defeat table. Both are the next step, tracked in issue \#86, and every statement elsewhere that sola fide "runs on three strands" remains true of \texttt{reformed} until then.

Nor does it encode the obedience reading of 3:36, where the opposite of the one who believes is the one who "does not obey" (ἀπειθῶν): no source arguing that Johannine faith therefore includes obedience has been verified yet.

\paragraph*{The lines}
\leavevmode\par
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{calvin\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}John\allowbreak{}Six}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Calvin's step from John to justification. John has no δικαιόω, so the bridge from eternal life through believing to justification by faith alone is an inference of its own. Rated \texttt{plausible}: no cited source grants the strand's grounds and denies the step, but the bridge rests on Calvin's comment rather than on an argument from John's vocabulary.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def calvinOnJohnSix : Source :=
  {
    primary :=
      Reference.work Bib.calvinJohn (Bib.Locus.adLoc Scripture.john6_29),
    tradition := Tradition.reformedProtestant,
    confidence := Confidence.plausible }
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{aquinas\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}Formed\allowbreak{}Faith}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Aquinas's step: faith living through charity is not bare trust. Rated \texttt{consensus}: Calvin concedes it — his trust is precisely a faith that brings nothing — and the two divide over the premise, not the inference.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def aquinasOnFormedFaith : Source :=
  {
    primary :=
      Reference.work Bib.aquinasJohn
        (Bib.Locus.sectionRef "cap. 6, lect. 3, n. 901"),
    tradition := Tradition.romanCatholic, confidence := Confidence.consensus }
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{johannine\allowbreak{}To\allowbreak{}Faith\allowbreak{}Alone}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The Johannine strand.} From the work God requires (6:28–29), and eternal life through believing (3:16–18, 3:36, 5:24, 20:31), to justification by faith alone — by way of the reading of that believing as trust.

\[(P_{34} \land P_{35} \land P_{36}) \rightarrow P_{24}\]
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{johannine\allowbreak{}Line}} --- Johannine strand (John 6:29)\par\nobreak\noindent
The Johannine line. Its texts are its own grounds, not shared prooftexts, because the strand is not yet part of \texttt{reformed}.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{34} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{35} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{36} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & (P_{34} \land P_{35} \land P_{36}) \rightarrow P_{24} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{24}
\end{align*}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{thomist\allowbreak{}Line}} --- Faith formed by charity (Aquinas on John 6:29)\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Aquinas's line.} The believing of 6:29 is faith living through charity, and so not the bare trust the strand needs. The denial is derived, not assumed: it follows from Aquinas's own reading of the verse.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{37} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{37} \rightarrow \lnot P_{36} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{36}
\end{align*}
\paragraph*{The packages}
\leavevmode\par
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{johannine\allowbreak{}Case}} --- Sola fide from John (6:28–29)\par\nobreak\noindent
Sola fide from John alone: the Johannine line, with the grounds and closing steps every Reformed strand shares.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{34} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{35} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{36} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & P_{4} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{17} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{9} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{20} \\
  \text{(12)} \quad & P_{21} \\
  \text{(13)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(14)} \quad & (P_{34} \land P_{35} \land P_{36}) \rightarrow P_{24} \\
  \text{(15)} \quad & (P_{20} \land P_{21}) \rightarrow P_{22} \\
  \text{(16)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5}) \rightarrow P_{25} \\
  \text{(17)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5} \land P_{22} \land P_{23}) \rightarrow P_{26} \\
  \text{(18)} \quad & (P_{24} \land P_{1}) \rightarrow P_{27} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{25} \land P_{26} \land P_{27}
\end{align*}
\emph{Grounded in scripture alone:} John 3:16–18, 3:36, 5:24, 20:31 — eternal life through believing in the Son.
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{thomist\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}John}} --- Aquinas on John 6:29 (faith formed by charity)\par\nobreak\noindent
Aquinas on John: every text the Johannine strand reads, and every shared ground, with its believing read as faith formed by charity. The rival the strand is written against.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{34} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{35} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & P_{37} \\
  \text{(4)} \quad & P_{1} \\
  \text{(5)} \quad & P_{2} \\
  \text{(6)} \quad & P_{3} \\
  \text{(7)} \quad & P_{4} \\
  \text{(8)} \quad & P_{5} \\
  \text{(9)} \quad & P_{17} \\
  \text{(10)} \quad & P_{9} \\
  \text{(11)} \quad & P_{20} \\
  \text{(12)} \quad & P_{21} \\
  \text{(13)} \quad & P_{23} \\
  \text{(14)} \quad & (P_{34} \land P_{35} \land P_{36}) \rightarrow P_{24} \\
  \text{(15)} \quad & P_{37} \rightarrow \lnot P_{36} \\
  \text{(16)} \quad & (P_{20} \land P_{21}) \rightarrow P_{22} \\
  \text{(17)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5}) \rightarrow P_{25} \\
  \text{(18)} \quad & (P_{1} \land P_{4} \land P_{5} \land P_{22} \land P_{23}) \rightarrow P_{26} \\
  \text{(19)} \quad & (P_{24} \land P_{1}) \rightarrow P_{27} \\
  \vdash \quad & P_{25} \land P_{26} \land P_{27}
\end{align*}
\emph{Grounded in scripture alone:} John 3:16–18, 3:36, 5:24, 20:31 — eternal life through believing in the Son.
\paragraph*{Results}
\leavevmode\par
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{johannine\_\allowbreak{}strand\_\allowbreak{}establishes}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{John alone delivers sola fide.} Grant that the believing of 6:29 is trust, and the work God requires is no work at all: sola fide follows from John without Paul, Luke or Acts.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem johannine_strand_establishes : Establishes johannineCase
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{thomist\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Aquinas's world: every text holds, and the believing of 6:29 is faith living through charity — so not bare trust, not faith alone, and salvation is not received through faith alone. Grace and "not by works" still hold: Aquinas excludes external works, and grants that faith is God's gift.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def thomistReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.johannineBelievingIsTrust => False
    | Claim.justificationByFaithAlone => False
    | Claim.salvationThroughFaith => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{johannine\_\allowbreak{}strand\_\allowbreak{}rests\_\allowbreak{}on\_\allowbreak{}believing\_\allowbreak{}as\_\allowbreak{}trust}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{The Johannine strand rests on believing as trust.} Grant Aquinas every text the strand reads, and read their believing as he does — faith living through charity — and sola fide no longer follows. So John's Gospel, like Paul's Galatians and Luke's σῴζω, carries the conclusion only through a reading: here, of what it is to believe \emph{in} him.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem johannine_strand_rests_on_believing_as_trust : ¬Establishes
    thomistOnJohn
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{johannine\allowbreak{}Case\_\allowbreak{}is\_\allowbreak{}satisfiable}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The Johannine case has a model, so \texttt{johannine\_\allowbreak{}strand\_\allowbreak{}establishes} is not vacuous.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem johannineCase_is_satisfiable : Satisfiable johannineCase.premises
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\paragraph*{Luther's answer}
\leavevmode\par
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}Galatians\allowbreak{}Three}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Luther's step, on Galatians 3:11–12: the law commands charity, and the law is not of faith, so faith formed by charity would justify by the law. Rated \texttt{disputed}: Aquinas grants both grounds — the text, and that the law commands love — and denies the conclusion, holding that justifying faith is formed by charity.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def lutherOnGalatiansThree : Source :=
  {
    primary :=
      Reference.work Bib.lutherGalatians
        (Bib.Locus.adLoc { book := Book.galatians, chapter := 3, verse := 12 }),
    supporting :=
      [Reference.work Bib.lutherGalatians
          (Bib.Locus.adLoc
            { book := Book.galatians, chapter := 3, verse := 11 })],
    tradition := Tradition.reformedProtestant,
    confidence := Confidence.disputed }
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\allowbreak{}Line}} --- Luther on Galatians 3:11–12 (against faith formed by charity)\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Luther's line.} From Galatians 3:11–12 and the law's command of love to the denial of Aquinas's reading: the believing that justifies is not faith formed by charity, since charity is what the law commands and the law is not of faith.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{38} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{39} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & (P_{38} \land P_{39}) \rightarrow \lnot P_{37} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{37}
\end{align*}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}Galatians}} --- Luther on Galatians 3:11–12 (against faith formed by charity)\par\nobreak\noindent
Luther's answer to Aquinas as an argument: what Aquinas grants, with Luther's step, against the reading of 6:29 as faith formed by charity.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{38} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{39} \\
  \text{(3)} \quad & (P_{38} \land P_{39}) \rightarrow \lnot P_{37} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{37}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{galatians\allowbreak{}Texts\allowbreak{}Alone}} --- Galatians 3:11–12 and the law's command of love, without Luther's step\par\nobreak\noindent
The same grounds without Luther's step: Galatians 3:11–12, and that the law commands love.

\begin{align*}
  \text{(1)} \quad & P_{38} \\
  \text{(2)} \quad & P_{39} \\
  \vdash \quad & \lnot P_{37}
\end{align*}
\emph{No premise here rests on scripture alone.}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\_\allowbreak{}answers\_\allowbreak{}aquinas\_\allowbreak{}from\_\allowbreak{}galatians}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Luther answers Aquinas from Galatians 3.} Grant what Aquinas grants — Galatians 3:11–12, and that the law commands love — and Luther's step, and the believing of John 6:29 is not faith formed by charity: a faith that justified because charity formed it would justify by the law, and the law is not of faith. What this removes is Aquinas's reading; it does not by itself establish Calvin's.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem luther_answers_aquinas_from_galatians : Establishes lutherOnGalatians
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\_\allowbreak{}answer\_\allowbreak{}rests\_\allowbreak{}on\_\allowbreak{}his\_\allowbreak{}step}}\par\nobreak\noindent
\textbf{Luther's answer rests on his step.} The texts alone do not exclude Aquinas's reading: in Aquinas's world Galatians 3:11–12 holds, the law commands love, and the believing of 6:29 is faith formed by charity. What decides it is whether faith formed by charity counts as justification "by the law" — Luther's step, which Aquinas denies.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem luther_answer_rests_on_his_step : ¬Establishes galatiansTextsAlone
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\allowbreak{}Reading}}\par\nobreak\noindent
The Reformed world, in which the believing of John 6:29 is not faith formed by charity, and everything else holds.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
def lutherReading : Valuation Claim :=
  fun a =>
    match a with
    | Claim.johannineBelievingIsFormedByCharity => False
    | x => True
\end{verbatim}}
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{\texttt{luther\allowbreak{}On\allowbreak{}Galatians\_\allowbreak{}is\_\allowbreak{}satisfiable}}\par\nobreak\noindent
Luther's package has a model, so \texttt{luther\_\allowbreak{}answers\_\allowbreak{}aquinas\_\allowbreak{}from\_\allowbreak{}galatians} is not vacuous.

{\small\begin{verbatim}
theorem lutherOnGalatians_is_satisfiable : Satisfiable
    lutherOnGalatians.premises
-- axioms: propext, Quot.sound
\end{verbatim}}
\clearpage
\section*{Sola scriptura}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Sola scriptura}

\subsection*{Arguments.SolaScriptura — scripture as the sole infallible rule of faith}
The formal principle of the Reformation. Sola fide is a claim \emph{within} scripture's teaching; sola scriptura is a claim \emph{about} scripture's authority, and so must answer objections the material principle never faces.

\subsubsection*{What the dispute is not about}
\textbf{It is not about whether doctrine develops.} Both Rome and the Protestant position encoded here hold that the deposit is closed while understanding of it deepens — \texttt{doctrine\allowbreak{}Develops\allowbreak{}Without\allowbreak{}New\allowbreak{}Revelation} is shared ground, held by Newman and by Mathison alike. The disagreement is entirely over what \emph{authenticates} a development: derivability from scripture, or the authority of the developing body. An encoding that opposed a static Protestantism to a developing Rome would describe a dispute neither party is having.

\subsubsection*{Four positions, not two}
Mathison's taxonomy, after Oberman, separates what the seed encoding collapsed:

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.45\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.45\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Position} & \textbf{What it holds} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\textbf{Tradition 0} & Tradition carries no binding authority; creeds inform without norming \\
\textbf{Tradition I} & Tradition is ministerial — real authority, fallible, subordinate to scripture \\
\textbf{Tradition II} & Two coordinate sources of revelation, canonised at Trent \\
\textbf{Tradition III} & The magisterium is the one real source, resting on Vatican I \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
Orthodoxy is a fifth thing again, and not a variant of Rome: authority rests in the mind of the Church, expressed in the consensus of the Fathers and in conciliar decisions received by the whole Church. Florovsky rejects the two-sources model outright. The seed grounded a single "Catholic/Orthodox" line on \texttt{magisterium\allowbreak{}Is\allowbreak{}Infallible}, which attributes to Orthodoxy a premise it does not hold.

\texttt{tradition0\_\allowbreak{}establishes} is the result that most repays the split: the position Mathison calls "solo scriptura" and judges unbiblical and unworkable reaches the conclusion too, once stated by Geisler — who holds it — rather than by its critic.

\subsubsection*{Three objections, which do not reduce to one another}
\textbf{Self-refutation.} If a doctrine binds only when scripture teaches it, and scripture does not teach sola scriptura, the position fails by its own standard.

\textbf{The canon objection.} If the canon is known through the Church's reception, an authority outside scripture is needed to identify scripture. Augustine supplies the classical form: \emph{ego vero Evangelio non crederem, nisi me catholicae Ecclesiae commoveret auctoritas}.

\textbf{The interpretive-authority regress.} Cross and Judisch argue that the individual retains ultimate interpretive authority, exercised indirectly by choosing which body to submit to, so Tradition I is not principled distinct from Tradition 0 — "when I submit (so long as I agree), the one to whom I submit is me."

Encoding objections that tell against the position is not a concession; the project's ground rules require rival readings to be encoded with the same care as the ones argued for.

\subsubsection*{The hinge does two jobs, and they come apart}
The seed asserted in prose that the dispute reduces to \texttt{sola\allowbreak{}Scriptura\allowbreak{}Is\allowbreak{}Taught\allowbreak{}By\allowbreak{}Scripture}. Split into the two roles that premise actually plays, the claim is both weaker and sharper than that.

It is \textbf{not} load-bearing for reaching the conclusion: \texttt{hinge\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}load\_\allowbreak{}bearing\_\allowbreak{}for\_\allowbreak{}conclusion} shows the eliminative line delivering the sole rule without it. It \textbf{is} load-bearing within the classical strand taken alone.

And against the self-refutation objection it is one of \emph{two} answers, not the only one. The objection's step is a conjunction, so either conjunct may be denied: \texttt{classical\_\allowbreak{}answer\_\allowbreak{}blocks\_\allowbreak{}self\_\allowbreak{}refutation} denies that scripture fails to teach the principle, and \texttt{scoping\_\allowbreak{}blocks\_\allowbreak{}self\_\allowbreak{}refutation} denies instead that the bindingness rule governs a claim about where binding doctrine comes from — the final-arbiter reading, on which scripture is not the only arbiter but the last one. The objection succeeds only against a position refusing both.

\textbf{Redundancy does not defend a position here}, which is where this argument parts company with \texttt{Sola\allowbreak{}Fide}. There, a second strand defeated an attack on a premise. These objections are not premise attacks: each independently delivers the negation of the conclusion, so a further route to the conclusion does nothing against them. What answers an objection is denying one of \emph{its} grounds.

\subsubsection*{The parity reply, and what it is worth}
Kruger on the canon and Mathison on interpretive authority make the same move: concede the circularity, deny that it discriminates between the positions. What the move achieves is that the disputed proposition comes out \emph{independent} of the reply's grounds: neither it nor its denial follows, because two readings satisfy those grounds and disagree about it. \texttt{parity\_\allowbreak{}leaves\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}canon\_\allowbreak{}open} and \texttt{parity\_\allowbreak{}leaves\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}distinction\_\allowbreak{}open} are those results.

Each used to be a \emph{pair} — one result that the reply blocks the objection, another that it establishes nothing — and the pair is what hid a defect. Over Barrett's parity reply to Geisler the two halves asked about different propositions while being presented as complements; \texttt{parity\_\allowbreak{}leaves\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}defeat\_\allowbreak{}open} is the claim that was meant, and \texttt{circle\_\allowbreak{}parity\_\allowbreak{}concedes\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}charge} is the second question restated, since that reply does not merely fail to clear the charge of circularity but grants it as one of its own grounds.

The replies neutralise; they do not establish. \texttt{compatibility\_\allowbreak{}does\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}establish\_\allowbreak{}criterion} records the same lesson in \texttt{Born\allowbreak{}Of\allowbreak{}AVirgin}, for a different argument and a different reply — and \emph{not} as an independence claim, because its two results range over different premise sets.

\subsubsection*{The circle inside Protestantism}
\texttt{circle\_\allowbreak{}leaves\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}consensus\_\allowbreak{}open} encodes Geisler's charge against Tradition I: the creedal consensus is said to rest on scripture's clarity, while scripture's clear sense is said to be unobtainable without that consensus. Both legs in place, neither end is settled either way — a cycle of implications is satisfied outright by a valuation on which every node in it is false, and equally by one on which every node is true. \texttt{circle\_\allowbreak{}leaves\_\allowbreak{}the\_\allowbreak{}perspicuity\_\allowbreak{}open} says the same of the other end.

That charge is made from inside the Reformation, against the position this module encodes as the Protestant one. It is conditional like every result here: deny either leg and the circle is not there.

\subsubsection*{A limitation worth stating}
The final-arbiter reading turns on a distinction between \textbf{doctrine}, which requires scriptural adjudication, and \textbf{practice} neither commanded nor forbidden, which does not. That is a \emph{scope} distinction, and this fragment is propositional: it cannot quantify. The distinction is carried by atom design and by this docstring, not by the logic. What the encoding shows is what follows given the distinction, not that the distinction can be drawn.

\subsubsection*{Where things are}
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.45\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.45\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{File} & \textbf{Contents} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\texttt{Atoms.lean} & the \texttt{Claim} atoms \\
\texttt{Sources.lean} & \texttt{cite}, with each position cited from a source that holds it \\
\texttt{Lines.lean} & the inference steps, the lines, and the replies as substituted grounds \\
\texttt{Packages.lean} & the positions, the objections, and the variants the results refute \\
\texttt{Results.lean} & every \texttt{@[headline]} result, with its trust base \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\subsection*{Arguments.SolaScriptura.Atoms — the atomic claims}
Four positions, three objections and two parity replies, so the atom list is longer than most. It is grouped by what each atom belongs to rather than by kind, because the groups are what the lines are built from.

Two atoms are worth flagging. \texttt{scripture\allowbreak{}Is\allowbreak{}Infallible} is granted by every party to the dispute and was absent from the seed encoding, which concluded that scripture is the sole \emph{infallible} rule without ever saying that scripture is infallible; the eliminative line needs it stated. And \texttt{thessalonians2\_\allowbreak{}15\allowbreak{}Tradition\allowbreak{}Binding} is a datum both sides must accommodate rather than a Roman premise — the seed filed it as the Catholic counter-text, which left the Protestant package with no answer to it.

\subsection*{Arguments.SolaScriptura.Sources — citation and classification for every atom}
\texttt{cite} is total, so an uncited atom does not compile.

Where the positions disagree about an atom they are given the source that actually holds the claim: the Orthodox premise cites Florovsky and Ware rather than a Protestant account of Orthodoxy, and Tradition 0 cites Geisler rather than Mathison's description of it — Geisler's principal complaint being that the description is a caricature.

\subsection*{The claims, numbered}
\begin{longtable}{@{}r >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.30\textwidth} l >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.19\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.21\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
 & claim & kind & held & source \\
\midrule
\endhead
$P_{1}$ & 2 Timothy 3:16 — all scripture is God-breathed and profitable & textual & Reformed Protestant, well supported & 2 Tim 3:16; \cite[I.vii.1]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{2}$ & 2 Timothy 3:17 — that the man of God may be complete, equipped for every good work & textual & Reformed Protestant, well supported & 2 Tim 3:17; \cite[I.vii.1]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{3}$ & Mark 7:8–13 — Jesus rebukes tradition that nullifies God's command & textual & Reformed Protestant, well supported & Mark 7:8-13; \cite[IV.x.8]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{4}$ & Acts 17:11 — the Bereans tested apostolic preaching against scripture & textual & Reformed Protestant, well supported & Acts 17:11; \cite[I.vii.2]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{5}$ & 2 Thessalonians 2:15 — hold to the traditions taught by word or letter\textsuperscript{\dag} & textual & Christian, hist.-gramm., consensus & 2 Thess 2:15 \\
$P_{6}$ & Scripture is infallible & theological & Christian, hist.-gramm., consensus & \cite[ch. 5]{grudem-systematic-theology-1994}; \cite[I.vii]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{7}$ & Scripture is a sufficient rule of faith & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[ch. 8]{grudem-systematic-theology-1994}; \cite{webster-holy-scripture-2003}; \cite{bavinck-prolegomena-2003}; \cite[I.vii]{calvin-institutes-1960}; \cite[I.6]{westminster-confession-1647} \\
$P_{8}$ & Scripture is clear on what is necessary for salvation & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[I.vii.5]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{9}$ & Scripture is the sole infallible rule of faith & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[I.vii–ix]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{10}$ & Scripture itself teaches that scripture is the sole infallible rule & interpretive & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[I.vii.4]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{11}$ & A doctrine is binding only if scripture teaches it & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[IV.x.8]{calvin-institutes-1960} \\
$P_{12}$ & No candidate rule of faith other than scripture is infallible & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[I.6]{westminster-confession-1647}; \cite{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{13}$ & Tradition is a coordinate source of revelation alongside scripture & theological & Roman Catholic, well supported & \cite[Trent, Session IV (1546), Decree on Sacred Books and Traditions]{tanner-decrees-1990}; \cite{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{14}$ & The magisterium is an infallible interpreter of scripture & theological & Roman Catholic, well supported & \cite[Vatican I (1870), Pastor Aeternus, ch. 4]{tanner-decrees-1990}; \cite{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{15}$ & The mind of the Church, in the consensus of the Fathers and in received conciliar decisions, infallibly interprets scripture & theological & Eastern Orthodox, well supported & \cite{florovsky-bible-church-tradition-1972}; \cite{ware-orthodox-church-1993} \\
$P_{16}$ & The canon is known through the Church's reception of it & historical & Roman Catholic, consensus & \cite[Augustine, Contra epistolam Manichaei 5.6]{npnf1-04-1887}; \cite[Trent, Session IV (1546), Decree on Sacred Books and Traditions]{tanner-decrees-1990} \\
$P_{17}$ & Identifying the canon requires an infallible authority & theological & Roman Catholic, disputed & \cite[Trent, Session IV (1546), Decree on Sacred Books and Traditions]{tanner-decrees-1990} \\
$P_{18}$ & A rival's own foundational authority is self-authenticating too, so the circularity charge does not discriminate & interpretive & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[ch. 2]{kruger-canon-revisited-2012} \\
$P_{19}$ & Tradition carries real but fallible — ministerial — authority & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001}; \cite{oberman-dawn-1986} \\
$P_{20}$ & Sola scriptura differs in principle from solo scriptura & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[ch. 9]{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{21}$ & The individual retains ultimate interpretive authority, exercised indirectly by choosing which body to submit to & theological & Roman Catholic, disputed & \cite[§ III]{cross-judisch-interpretive-authority-2009} \\
$P_{22}$ & Choosing which authority to submit to is itself private judgement, whichever authority is chosen & interpretive & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[ch. 9]{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{23}$ & The bindingness rule governs first-order doctrine, not a claim about where binding doctrine comes from & theological & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[I.6]{westminster-confession-1647}; \cite{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{24}$ & A practice neither commanded nor forbidden in scripture is permitted without scriptural warrant & theological & Reformed Protestant, well supported & \cite[Formula of Concord, Solid Declaration X]{kolb-wengert-book-of-concord-2000}; \cite[I.6]{westminster-confession-1647} \\
$P_{25}$ & Creeds are informative without being normative & theological & Christian, hist.-gramm., disputed & \cite[120]{geisler-review-mathison-2005} \\
$P_{26}$ & The historical-grammatical method alone suffices to obtain the meaning of scripture on essential doctrine & interpretive & Christian, hist.-gramm., disputed & \cite[120–121]{geisler-review-mathison-2005} \\
$P_{27}$ & The creedal consensus is hermeneutically necessary for understanding scripture & interpretive & Reformed Protestant, disputed & \cite[275]{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
$P_{28}$ & Doctrine develops without new revelation: the deposit is closed & theological & Christian, hist.-gramm., well supported & \cite{newman-development-1845}; \cite{mathison-shape-sola-scriptura-2001} \\
